みずほ経済 EXPRESS

みずほ GDP ナウ（2026 年 10 月中旬時点） 
～7～9 月期 GDP は前期比＋0.03％（年率＋0.10％）と推計～

調査部  シニア日本経済エコノミスト 越山祐資 
同             エコノミスト  阿部大樹 
080-1069-4835 yusuke.koshiyama@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 10 月中旬時点の「みずほ GDP ナウ」は 7～9 月期 GDP を前期比＋0.10％と予測 
みずほ総合研究所では景気動向をタイムリーに把握するべく、浦沢（2023）等を参考にGDPナウキ
ャスティングに取り組んできた。これを「みずほGDPナウ」と呼称することとし、太田他（2024）で
はデータドリブン予測である「みずほGDPナウ」が民間予測平均並みの予測精度を確保できることを
示した。その上で、酒井他（2024）をはじめとして、月次経済指標を用いたGDPナウキャスティング
の結果を紹介してきたところである。 
本稿では10月中旬時点までに得られる月次経済指標を用い、 「みずほGDPナウ」 による7～9月期GDP
の予測結果を紹介する。予測に用いたデータは、8月分の鉱工業生産、 消費活動指数、 所定外労働時間、
消費財出荷指数、第3次産業活動指数、9月分の中小企業景況調査（売上げ見通しDI）である1。図表1
には、予測に使用した各月次経済指標の推移を示している。 
図表 1 各月次経済指標の推移

（注）青字は改善、赤字は悪化を示す

日本銀行「消費活動指数」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 2

2

モデルによる10月中旬時点における7 ～9月期実質GDP成長率の予測値は、図表2のとおり前期比＋
0.03％ （年率＋0.10％）となった。 他機関による予測では、東京財団政策研究所による10月15日時点の
ナウキャスティングが同＋0.29％（年率＋1.18％）、日本経済研究センターが公表した10月のESPフォ
ーキャスト調査（回答期間：2026年9月30日～10月7日、回答者37名）における民間予測値平均が前期
比年率＋0.08％となっている。「みずほGDPナウ」の予測値は、民間コンセンサスよりやや高いもの
の、概ね近い伸びを予測していると言える。 
なお、多くの民間エコノミストが保守的な見方をしている理由の一つには、GDP統計作成上のテク
ニカルな要因がある。4～6月期には、 中東情勢悪化を背景に原油 ・ ナフサ輸入が大幅に減少したほか、
石油化学製品の供給途絶懸念から幅広い企業で在庫積み増しの動きが生じた。やや直感に反するが、
いずれもGDPに対しては押し上げ要因となる。 そのため、4～6月期GDPは前期比＋0.36％ （年率＋1.45％）
とプラス成長になったものの、 成長率が示すほど景気の実態は強くなかったとみるのが自然であろう。
反対に、7～9月期については、4～6月期の反動で輸入増・在庫減の動きが顕在化し、GDPには一定の
下方圧力がかかる可能性がある。みずほ総合研究所が9月に公表したレポート2でも、こうしたテクニ
カル要因を考慮の上、7～9月期GDPは横ばい圏の伸びにとどまると予想している。 
■ 「みずほ GDP ナウ」による予測値下方修正の主因は生産・消費関連指標の悪化 
「みずほGDPナウ」 による7～9月期のGDP（1次速報値） の予測にあたっては10月中旬までに公表さ
れた月次経済指標を使って予測値を4回更新した。図表3では、予測値の更新過程と各月次経済指標の
寄与度を示している3。予測値が下方修正された主因は、8月の鉱工業生産、 消費財出荷指数の低下だ。
特に鉱工業生産指数については、7月末に発生した熊本地震による影響が下押し要因になったとみら
れる。 大手自動車メーカーで工場の操業が停止した影響で自動車が前月比▲6.8％と大幅に減産したほ
か、 電子部品 ・ デバイスも同▲1.9％の減産となった。7月調査時点の生産予測指数は前月比＋3.1％（ 補
図表 2 各機関の実質GDP成長率予測値 図表 3 予測値の改定過程と各経済指標の寄与度

キャスティング」、日本経済研究センター「ESPフォーキャス
ト調査」等より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

質GDP成長率予測値。棒グラフは予測値に対する各経済指標の
寄与度を示す。「みずほGDPナウ」は各経済指標の結果を公表
前に予測しており （モデル予測値） 、 寄与度はモデル予測値と
実績値の乖離幅から計算される。 なお、 各経済指標の確報を受
けて指標が改定された場合も寄与度に反映される

### Page 3

3

正値ベース）と増産が見込まれていたが、大幅に下振れた格好だ。一方、世界的に旺盛なAI・データ
センター関連需要を背景に、半導体製造装置を含む生産用機械などが増産したものの、熊本地震の影
響を打ち返せなかった。消費財出荷指数の低下については、4～5月の耐久財特需が一巡したことで反
動減の動きが生じた。非耐久財の出荷は前月から増加したものの、7月の落ち込みも踏まえると、 基調
としては力強さを欠いていると言えよう。長雨や低温、といった天候不順により生鮮野菜価格が上昇
しているほか、食肉も値上がりが大きく、こうした手に取る機会が多い品目の価格上昇が家計の節約
志向を強め、個人消費の下押し圧力となっているとみられる。9月についても、食品のほか、原油・石
油化学製品等のコスト高の影響で幅広い品目の価格上昇が加速する見込みであり、個人消費は冴えな
い動きが続く可能性がある。 
以上のように、7～9月期成長率は低い伸びに抑えられる公算が大きい。前述のとおり原油・ナフサ
の輸入増、 企業の在庫調整といった動きはGDP統計上、成長率に対してマイナスに寄与する。加えて、
熊本地震による生産減や天候要因による消費支出抑制の動きが下押し要因になる見込みだ。 
次回の 「みずほGDPナウ」については、11月16日に公表される7～9月期GDP1次速報を挟んで、10～
12月期GDPのリアルタイム予測に取り組む。10月分の鉱工業生産や消費活動指数の結果等を踏まえて
推計し、12月中旬頃のレポート発刊を予定している。原油高等を受けた物価上昇圧力の強まりや堅調
なAI・データセンター関連需要など、プラス・マイナス双方の影響が交錯する中、ナウキャストによ
る景気動向の把握はより重要なものとなるだろう。

［参考文献］

けて～」 、内閣府経済社会総合研究所「経済分析」第208号

### Page 4

1 ステップワイズ法により使用データを採択した。詳細は太田ほか（2024）を参照されたい。今後のモデルの予測精度のパフォ
ーマンス評価等を踏まえ、採択するデータについては見直しを行う可能性がある。 
2 詳細については、服部直樹「年率＋1.4％と 1 次速報から小幅に上方修正（4～6 月期2 次QE）」（2026 年9 月8 日）を参照され
たい。 
3 太田他（2024）で示した枠組みと同様、図表 2 の折れ線が各時点における実質GDP 成長率の予測値であり、月次の経済指標が
新たに公表されたり改定されたりすることで予測値が更新される。棒グラフは、予測値の改定幅、すなわち前回予測との差を各
月次経済指標で寄与度分解したもので、寄与度を合計するとモデル予測値の改定幅と一致する。なお、 「みずほGDP ナウ」は各
月次経済指標についても公表前に予測値を計算しており、各指標の寄与度はモデル予測値と実績値の乖離から計算されることか
ら、寄与度の符号は経済指標の変化の方向とは必ずしも一致しない。

## Document 036: 20261019_投資週報_通膨升溫增添波動，台積財報展現韌性

Source PDF: all report pdf/20261019_投資週報_通膨升溫增添波動，台積財報展現韌性.pdf
Report source: Yuanta (投資週報)
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同
意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 19 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國 9 月通膨升溫，Fed 年底前再升息機率仍高： 美國 9 月 CPI 年增 3.7%，高於 8 月的
3.4%；核心 CPI 年增率亦由 2.4%升至 2.6%，顯示通膨壓力不只來自能源，核心價格亦有回
升。隔日的 PPI 年增率則由 5.6%升至 6.3%，反映上游成本壓力仍強。通膨重新升溫，使Fed
即使面對就業降溫，也難以快速轉向寬鬆，市場對年底前再度升息的預期仍高。
◼ 台積電財報創新高，AI 需求強勁但市場轉向檢視融資風險：台積電 10/15 公布其第三季淨利

達 7,631 億元、年增 69%，連續第六季刷新紀錄，第三季美元營收 467.2 億美元，並預估第
四季營收將升至 523～543 億美元 ； 公司同時將2026 全年營收成長預估提高至約 44%， 反映
AI 晶片與先進製程需求仍極為強勁。然而，高利率環境使市場開始檢視 AI 資料中心業者的槓
桿與融資能力，CoreWeave 約 500 億美元債務引發信用疑慮，引發市場波動。
◼ 中東衝突再度推高能源風險 ， 油價高檔加劇全球通膨壓力 ：胡塞武裝上週攻擊沙烏地阿美設施 ，
伊朗亦表示採取新的海上攻擊策略，使荷莫茲海峽與波斯灣航運風險再度升高。10/16 布蘭特
原油與西德洲原油期貨雙雙收在近三週高點。
市場展望
◼ 科技股中期趨勢料將不變 ， 但短線波動勢將加劇 ：近期市場同時面臨通膨升溫 、AI 資本支出疑
慮與重要科技財報公佈在即等不確定因素，短線風險偏好可能受到壓抑。在利率維持高檔及市
場評價偏高的環境下，市場短線料將隨財報結果與市場預期而出現較大波動。然而，上週台積
電與 ASML 財報結果整體優於預期 ， 可見AI 相關企業營運與資本支出尚未出現全面轉弱跡象 ，

科技產業基本面仍具一定支撐，惟後續宜更加重視企業獲利、現金流及資本支出變動及科技子
產業的表現分化現象。
◼ 中天期美債與投資級債債券兼顧息收與利率風險：美國經濟仍具韌性 ， 加上政府與企業發債供
給維持高檔，可能使市場利率持續處於較高水準。雖然投資級債信用利差位於相對低檔，但企
業獲利、現金流及償債能力仍具支撐，加上較高的美國公債基準利率，使投資級債的利息收益
仍具吸引力。相較之下，短天期債券面臨未來利率下降後的再投資風險，長天期債券則較容易
受到公債供給增加 、 期限溢酬及利率波動影響 ， 因此中天期美國公債與投資債券較能兼顧息收
與利率風險。

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國 9 月通膨數據高於市場預期，CPI 月增 0.6%、年增 3.7%；核心 CPI 月增
0.3%、年增 2.6%。同月 PPI 月增 1.3%，創 2022 年 3 月以來最大增幅，年增
6.3%， 顯示通膨壓力已逐步由能源價格擴散至更廣泛領域 。 數據公布後 ， 市場一
度提高對聯準會 10 月升息的預期，但隨後多位官員釋出審慎訊號，使升息押注
降溫 。 聯準會理事鮑曼表示 ， 決策前仍有時間觀察先前升息效果 ； 舊金山聯準銀
行總裁戴莉則認為 10 月無立即升息之必要。而褐皮書顯示，美國就業市場維持
「低招聘、低裁員」格局，而高能源價格帶來的通膨壓力仍值得關注。
⚫ 中國 9 月通膨持續回升，CPI 年增 0.9%，創近三個月高點；PPI 年增 5.0%，創
逾四年最大漲幅，顯示通縮壓力進一步減輕。主要受能源與原物料價格上漲帶
動，且 9 月進出口表現優於預期，AI 產業鏈需求與貿易環境改善提供支撐；不

過，信貸增長仍仰賴政府發債與季底效應，內需與民間融資動能仍偏弱。
⚫ 日本銀行審議委員小枝淳子表示 ， 不宜對升息過於保守 ， 並認為政策利率仍有進
一步上調空間。總裁植田和男則強調維持2%通膨目標的重要性。整體而言，日
本央行持續釋出偏鷹派訊號，有助支撐日圓表現。
⚫ 歐洲央行管委溫施表示 ， 在能源價格波動下 ，為了控制通膨仍不排除進一步升息
可能，總裁拉加德則認為法國債務問題應透過財政政策解決， 歐洲央行官員普遍
認為能源價格的潛在波動依然是推升通膨並使其居高不下的主要風險來源。。
⚫ 澳洲央行會議紀錄指出，能源價格上漲與需求韌性可能使通膨更具黏性，保留
進一步升息空間。然而 9 月就業數據意外疲弱，市場大幅下調升息預期，目前
預估 11 月維持利率不變的機率接近 90%。

美元兌新台幣匯率走勢
Oct/19/2026
＜上週走勢＞
上週台幣匯價高點為 31.890，低點在 32.520，以 32.338 作收，較前一周收盤價
貶值 4 角 6.3 分。
上週台幣走貶 ， 周初 ， 外資延續前周大幅匯出的走勢 ， 雖逢高吸引出口商拋匯 ，
仍不敵外資匯出力道令台幣走貶 ； 週二台股重挫近千點 ， 外資大幅賣超台股1653 億
並匯出，台幣接連跌破 32.000、32.100 及 32.200，總成交量 71.3 億，創歷史新高；
周三台股收漲，外資雙向進出，台幣收斂部分跌幅；週四，受美國通膨高於預期影
響，早盤台幣跳空開低，台股跌逾2%，外資大幅賣超 3025 億，台幣跌破 31.400 關
卡 ， 總成交量91.2 億 ， 再創歷史新高 ； 週五外資持續匯出 ， 台幣貶至周內低點32.520，
終場在央行調節之下收在 32.338。
＜未來分析＞
預估本週台幣走勢偏弱。就國際市場而言，美國 CPI、PPI 雙雙高於預期，通

膨壓力仍在，儘管聯準會官員陸續發言表示不急於升息，淡化市場對於聯準會十月
升息預期，惟年底前聯準會仍有升息一碼空間，利率持穩於高位將給予國際美元支
撐；就台灣市場來看，市場對AI 產業資本支出、融資成本及信用風險產生疑慮，若
賣壓延續，將壓抑市場風險偏好，增加外資調整台股部位及匯出的壓力，令台幣面
臨持續貶值的壓力，惟出口商拋匯將抑制台幣貶值速度，綜合以上，預估本週台幣
走勢偏弱。
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.100-32.700

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪走升。上週人民幣中間價連續走升，週三中間價升至6.7249 創
三年八個月新高，惟市場靜待美國公布通膨數據，交投謹慎，人民幣僅小幅走升；
週三晚間，美國公布 9 月 CPI 年增率 3.7%、核心 CPI 年增 2.6%，雙雙高於預期，
帶動美元指數升至年內高點 102.945、 人民幣貶至周內低點6.7005； 然多位聯準會官
員發言表示聯準會不急於升息，令市場對於聯準會十月升息預期略有降溫，美元指
數自高點回落，激勵人民幣由貶轉升；臨近周末，聯準會主席華許於IMF 會議發言
並未指引十月聯準會政策利率走向，再度淡化市場對於聯準會十月升息預期，國際
美元走勢偏弱，人民幣走升觸及年內高點 6.6825，終場境外人民幣收在 6.6850，週
線升值 91 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6870，週線升值 60 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣震盪偏升。就國際市場來看，美國仍面臨高通膨、年底可能將
再次升息，利差因素支撐美元指數於高檔震盪，限縮人民幣升值空間；就中國國內
市場來看，中間價持續偏升將帶動人民幣走升，加以出口商實質拋匯需求將支撐人
民幣表現；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，匯價維持在五日均線之下交
投 ， 均線呈現空頭排列 ， 加以USD/CNH 有效跌破 6.6900 整數關卡 ， 顯示USD/CNH
空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣震盪偏升。

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 USD/JPY 先上後下。週初，受油價攀升推升美國通膨與利率預期帶動，美
元指數溫和走升創 6 月底以來新高，推升 USD/JPY 突破 158.50；週間，美國公布 9
月 CPI 高於預期，帶動美元指數升至 2025 年 5 月以來高點，也使 USD/JPY 最高來
到 158.94，然逢高獲利了結賣壓出籠使匯價回落；臨近週末，USD/JPY 一度下探至
157.53，隨後逢低買盤進場收復部分跌幅，終場收在 157.99，週線下跌 13 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、受美伊戰事推升汽柴油飆漲
與服務成本擴散影響，美國 9 月 CPI 及 PPI 月增漲幅均超出市場預期，加上初請失
業金人數連五週低於 20 萬人顯示勞動市場穩定，市場預期聯準會將於 12 月再次升
息，美日實質利差維持高檔，持續為USD/JPY 提供下檔支撐。二、被視為鴿派的日
本經濟財政大臣城內實明確表態，貨幣政策應交由日本央行自行判斷，並宣告日本
已擺脫長期通縮、轉型為增長導向經濟體。此番言論降低政治干預日銀進一步升息
的疑慮，在貨幣政策正常化的腳步下，預計將限縮 USD/JPY 的上行空間。三、就技
術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 匯價目前交投於月線及年線之間，各天期均線高度
糾結，多空雙方於 158 關卡附近拉鋸，在有效突破季線及年線反壓或跌破月線支撐
前 ， 仍以區間震盪看待。 綜合以上 ， 預估USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高 。

EUR/USD 震盪走弱；週間，因美國9 月 CPI 高於預期，市場重新評估聯準會年底前
升息腳步，導致 EUR/USD 跳水最低來到 1.1112；臨近週末，儘管美國 PPI 與零售
銷售數據偏熱，市場優先反映美債殖利率回落與風險資產重新定價，美元多頭獲利
了結帶動 EUR/USD 谷底反彈 ， 最高來到1.1269， 終場收在1.1239， 週線上漲42 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間偏空震盪，主要原因如下：一、法國央行月度展望報
告指出，法國第三季 GDP 預估僅微幅成長 0.1%，印證近期高能源的成本已實質拖
累歐元區核心成員國增長。同時，歐洲央行總裁拉加德明確拒絕出手干預法債，要
求法國透過自身財政政策化解赤字困境；在明年法國大選前的財政隱憂與基本面疲
弱下，預計將令歐元上檔持續承壓。二、儘管歐洲央行管委溫施與總裁拉加德保留
12 月升息預期，加上德國基建與軍費支出為歐元提供部分下檔保護，但市場穩固預
期聯準會年底前將再升息一次。在美國經濟韌性與高利差優勢主導下，預計將限縮
歐元反彈空間。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 自低點反彈並站回五日線
之上，然而中長期均線下彎仍維持空頭排列，RSI 指標亦處於 50 中軸下方，顯示整
體空方趨勢尚未扭轉，反彈至月線附近恐面臨較大賣壓。綜合以上，預估EUR/USD
本週呈現區間偏空震盪的可能性較高。

                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115. 10.12-115.10.16 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.67% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15109     2.020~2.025% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.88%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ，本週央行連續減發定存單使市場資金寬鬆 ， 雖外資大舉匯
出，資金供給仍充裕，帶動短率朝區間低位移動。 
週五央行標售 2 年期定存單 250 億元 ， 加權平均得標利率為1.58%， 低於市場
預期區間 1.60%-1.62%，較上期標售結果下跌 5.8 個基本點。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.67%~1.68%，利率大致持平於上週。 
二、 5 年期指標券 A15108 週二搓合成交在 1.88%，後隨美債利率滑落，加上本週
2 年期公債標債結果提振市場持券信心，帶動 10 年期指標券 A15109 週四向
下成交在 2.02%~2.025%。 
在銀行去化短期資金及補充 LCR 之需求下 ， 週三2 年期公債得標利率為1.65%，
落在市場預估區間下緣 ， 投標倍數回升至2.86 倍 ， 銀行業得標比重上升至88%。
股市承壓帶動避險需求，美債利率大幅回檔，促使台債交投偏空氛圍轉趨和
緩 ， 惟在央行維持緊縮貨幣政策下 ， 買盤積極追價的意願不強 ， 預期短線台債
殖利率呈區間格局的可能性較高，關注下週 10 年券標售結果，預估 5 年券區
間為 1.85%~1.90%，10 年券區間為 1.98%~2.03%。

1

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
企業活動維持擴張，消費信心與房市疲弱，通膨及關稅風險仍存 
經濟 
⚫ 10 月美國綜合 PMI 產出指數初值由 58.4 降至 57.6，企業活動仍強勁擴
張，內需及出口帶動訂單與就業成長；惟油價、關稅及供應鏈瓶頸推升
成本，售價漲幅創 2022 年 8 月以來新高，通膨壓力持續升溫，後續須
關注聯準會政策動向。 
⚫ 9 月美國住宅市場續顯疲弱，建築許可及新屋開工分別年減 3.0%及
7.4%，反映住宅投資動能放緩。儘管多戶住宅許可與完工數回升，但整
體營建活動仍偏弱，後續仍須關注房貸利率、住房負擔能力及營建成本
對市場復甦的影響。 
⚫ 密西根大學公布之 10 月美國消費者信心指數降至 48.0，月減 0.2%、年
減 10.4%，顯示民眾對目前經濟環境仍缺乏信心，雖然對未來景氣的看
法略有改善，但高物價與借貸成本仍讓消費者感到壓力。此外，未來一
年及長期通膨預期分別升至 4.7%及 3.5%，反映民眾仍擔心物價持續上
漲。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 10 月 23 日川普宣布調整加拿大商品 50%關稅清單，10 月 30 日生效，
豁免部分紙製品、機械及包裝材料，並新增地毯、油漆等課稅項目。外
媒認為此舉與期中選舉有關，有助降低部分企業成本，但美加貿易摩擦
仍未化解。 
短評 
⚫ 10 月美國綜合產出指數雖略為下滑 ， 但仍維持高檔 ， 顯示內需與就業持
續支撐企業活動擴張。然而，消費者信心仍處低檔，反映高物價與借貸
成本持續壓抑購買意願，儘管民眾對未來景氣的看法略有改善，但新屋
開工及建築許可同步下滑，顯示消費與住宅投資前景仍面臨挑戰。另一
方面，美國調整部分加拿大商品關稅，雖有助減輕部分企業進口成本，
但雙邊貿易摩擦尚未化解，政策不確定性仍存。

### Page 3

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

2

歐 
元 
區 
法國動盪短期內難降溫，料將使央行保持審慎觀望態度 
經濟 ⚫ 歐元區 10 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)下降 1.3 點至 51.9，服務業、製造業 PMI 分別降 1.3、0.5 點至 51.7、52.9。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 歐洲議會代表團於 10 月 27 日至 29 日訪問中國，料將討論稀土及綠能
技術的依賴、貿易失衡，以及歐洲企業在中國面臨的不平等待遇問題。 
⚫ 法國抗爭活動規模逐漸擴大 ， 各大工會與抗議團體已宣布在 10 月 31 日
再度發起大規模遊行，並預告於 11 月 5 日舉行全國性大罷工。

兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處

一、 美國—企業活動維持擴張，消費信心與房市疲弱，通膨及關稅風險仍存 
標普全球(S&P Global)公布，2026 年 10 月美國綜合 PMI 產出指數(Composite 
PMI Output Index) 初值由上月的 58.4 降至 57.6，其中服務業採購經理人指數
(Services PMI)由 58.8 降至 57.9，製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI)則由
55.9 微升至 56.0，創 2022 年 5 月以來新高，企業活動雖略有放緩，但仍維持強
勁擴張。需求方面，內需穩健及出口改善帶動整體新訂單增速創逾 4 年半新高，
商品出口訂單亦為 2025 年 6 月以來首次增加；企業持續擴增人力，整體就業增
速為 2022 年 6 月以來次高。另一方面，未完成訂單增加、供應商交貨延遲加劇，
加上油價上漲及關稅推升能源、運輸與原物料成本，使企業投入成本維持高檔，
帶動售價漲幅升至 2022 年 8 月以來最高。儘管企業對未來一年營運前景的樂觀
程度略降，但仍處於今年相對高檔。整體而言，美國經濟在內外需及就業支撐下
維持擴張，惟供應鏈瓶頸與成本上升加劇通膨壓力，後續仍須關注物價走勢及聯
準會貨幣政策動向。 
密西根大學公布，2026 年 10 月美國消費者信心指數終值降至 48.0，略低於
上月的 48.1，月減 0.2%、年減 10.4%，並較今年 2 月下降約 15%，顯示消費者信
心持續處於低檔。分項來看，當前經濟狀況指數由 50.9 降至 46.7，月減 8.3%、
年減 20.3%；消費者預期指數則由 46.3 回升至 48.8，月增 5.4%、年減 3.0%，反
映民眾對未來景氣的看法略有改善，但整體信心仍偏弱。調查指出，高利率及購
買負擔壓力使耐久財購買條件下降 18%，拖累當前經濟狀況指數；未來一年企業
景氣預期雖改善約 14%，但仍低於去年同期 16%。通膨預期方面，未來一年通膨
預期由 4.6%升至 4.7%， 長期通膨預期亦由3.4%升至 3.5%， 雙雙連續第二個月攀
升，創今年 5 月以來最高水準。密西根大學指出，2025 年關稅政策衝擊及 2026
年伊朗衝突引發的能源價格上漲，使民眾通膨預期再度升溫，且長期預期已明顯
高於 2019 年及 2024 年水準。整體而言，10 月美國消費者信心大致持平，但高物
價與借貸成本仍壓抑購買意願，加上短期及長期通膨預期持續上升，可能增加物
價回穩的難度，後續仍須關注能源價格、家庭消費及通膨預期變化對聯準會貨幣
政策的影響。

2026 年 9 月美國住宅市場建築許可年化總數降至 140.0 萬戶，月減0.2%、年
減 3.0%，其中單戶住宅建築許可降至 84.5 萬戶，月減 4.0%、年減 4.8%，惟 5 戶
以上多戶住宅建築許可增至 50.3 萬戶，月增 5.9%、年增 1.0%，顯示不同住宅類
型開發規劃表現分歧。新屋開工年化數降至 122.1 萬戶，月減5.2%、年減 7.4%，
其中單戶住宅開工 85.9 萬戶，較去年同期仍增 2.6%，但多戶住宅開工降至 34.9
萬戶，年減25.9%，拖累整體營建動能。施工方面，在建住宅維持128.0 萬戶，與
上月持平但較去年減少 3.3%；完工年化數則回升至 119.2 萬戶，月增2.2%，主要
受多戶住宅完工月增 14.9%帶動，惟整體仍較去年同期減少 14.0%。整體而言，
美國住宅市場呈現建築許可及新屋開工下滑、在建規模持平及完工略有回升的態
勢，反映住宅營建活動仍偏弱，後續仍須關注房貸利率走勢、住房負擔能力及營
建成本變化對住宅市場復甦的影響。 
美國總統川普 10 月 23 日依據《1930 年關稅法》第 338 條簽署公告，調整部
分加拿大商品加徵 50%關稅的適用範圍，並自 10 月 30 日起生效。此次調整取消
膠合板、部分紙製品、機械與冷凍設備、包裝用木箱及紙箱、新鮮與乾燥海藻等
商品的額外關稅，同時新增閥門、地毯、油漆、洗髮精、可可粉、木製棺材、空
調設備及獨木舟等課稅項目，呈現局部鬆綁與新增課稅並行的政策方向。白宮表
示 ， 此舉旨在減輕加拿大貿易措施對美國企業造成的負擔 ， 並維護國內產業利益 。
由於緬因州農業及製造業高度依賴加拿大進口物資，共和黨參議員柯林斯 (Susan 
Collins)積極向白宮爭取關稅豁免 ， 外媒亦將此次調整與11 月 3 日期中選舉連結 。
整體而言，新措施有助降低部分美國企業的進口成本，但美加貿易摩擦尚未完全
化解，後續仍須關注雙邊談判及關稅政策對供應鏈的影響。 
二、歐元區—法國動盪短期內難降溫，料將使央行保持審慎觀望態度 
 歐元區 10 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)下降 1.3 點至 51.9，抵銷上月的強勁
增幅，但為連續五個月高於 50 的榮枯線，主要係受到法國大規模抗議活動的影
響，使法國綜合 PMI 下降 2.7 點至 48.4；此外，德國綜合PMI 下降 1 點至 52.8，
亦反映近期通膨壓力增加 ， 使民眾傾向保守消費 。 若以產業區分 ， 歐元區服務業 、
製造業 PMI 分別降 1.3、0.5 點至 51.7、52.9；惟德國製造業 PMI 上升至相當強勁
的 54.2，反映德國政府擴張軍備投資以及基礎建設之需求，持續推動製造業生產
動能。

法國抗爭活動規模逐漸擴大 ， 各大工會與抗議團體已宣布在 10 月 31 日再度
發起大規模遊行，並預告於 11 月 5 日舉行全國性大罷工，抗議主要訴求包括改
善公共服務品質及凍結物價上漲。然而，在財政壓力巨大且國會缺乏穩定執政多
數的情況下，法國政府既無法強力通過重大的財政改革，也難以快速推出安撫反
對派的妥協法案或者公共支出。故部分較為悲觀的機構認為此波動盪可能會延續
至 2027 年 4 月的法國總統大選。受法國局勢影響，近期歐元區各國殖利率普遍
呈現上升趨勢，並對景氣產生暫時性的抑制效果。在此背景下，市場預期歐洲央
行在 10 月 28 日的政策會議將採取觀望立場，後續關注歐洲央行是否將透過政策
訊號或流動性工具，為平息法國主權債務利率而採取行動。 
三、日本—貿易逆差及通膨壓力延續，內需仍具韌性 
 9 月出口雖維持強勁 ， 受半導體製造設備及汽車出口增加所帶動 ， 出口額年增
16.7%；惟進口增幅更為顯著，9 月進口額年增 18.9%，其中原油及液化天然氣進
口額分別大增 51.1%及 39.2%，主要反映中東局勢推升能源價格，以及日本尋求
替代能源供應來源的影響。由於進口額高於出口額，9 月貿易逆差達 5,365 億日
圓，為連續第 5 個月呈現逆差；累計 2026 上半財年（4 至 9 月）貿易逆差亦較上
年同期擴大 98.8%至 2.82 兆日圓。整體而言，出口並未明顯轉弱，近期貿易逆差
擴大主要反映能源進口成本攀升，使進口增幅持續高於出口。 
 9 月全國消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 2.1%，即使政府透過能源及教育補貼等措
施抑制物價，通膨仍高於2%，顯示實際通膨動能更為強勁。從物價結構來看，石
油、原物料及工資成本上升已逐步傳導至終端價格，漲價範圍由家具、醫療用品
及休閒用品等商品 ， 進一步擴散至房屋維護 、 公共運輸 、 住宿及外食等服務項目 ，
顯示企業成本轉嫁範圍逐步擴大，通膨壓力亦由商品向服務領域擴散。隨通膨持
續高於 2%且漲價範圍擴大 ， 亦為日本央行進一步推動貨幣政策正常化提供支撐 。 
 10 月綜合 PMI 持平於 52.3，顯示經濟仍維持穩定擴張，其中製造業 PMI 下
降 0.8 點至 53.3，產出指數亦降至 3 月以來最低，主要反映新訂單及出口訂單成
長放緩，加上稀土金屬等原料供應受限，對生產形成一定壓力，惟生產與訂單仍
處擴張區間，尚未顯示製造業景氣明顯轉弱；服務業 PMI 回升 0.7 點至 52.0，新
業務及出口訂單同步改善，顯示服務需求仍具韌性，為支撐景氣的重要動能。另
一方面，能源、薪資及原物料成本居高不下，使投入價格壓力持續偏高，並逐漸

四、中國—第三季經濟成長率回升，但前景仍較為不利 
2026 年第三季 GDP 年增率初估值為 4.5%，較第二季的 4.3%上揚 0.2 個百分
點；累計 1-9 月 GDP 年增率則為 4.6%。第三季的二、三級產業年增率分別較第
二季上揚 0.4、0.1 個百分點至 3.4%， 其中製造業 、 批發零售業年增率分別達5.9%、
2.3%為主要動能 ， 抵銷建築業年減4.4%等拖累因素影響 。 整體而言 ， 第三季GDP
年增率上揚有部分受到中秋國慶連假落在 9 月末段(去年同期則於 10 月 1 日開
始)，致基期效果發揮部分影響，相關消費動能仍可能會在 10 月出現暫時性的回
落。 
在需求指標方面，(1)1-9 月累計零售額年增率為 1.1%，持平於 1-8 月的累計
年增率。其中 9 月零售額年增率增加 0.7 個百分點至 1.1%，部分受到新手機上市
帶動買氣，9 月通訊器材消費年增率高達 31.1%，加上中秋國慶長假帶動食品消
費年增率增加 1 個百分點至 2.1%所致 ；(2)1-9 月固定資產投資年減幅維持於 7.2%，
反映供應鏈移轉、政府主導去化過剩產能等因素導致民間投資需求急遽萎縮；萎
縮產業包含化工、醫藥、專用設備、汽車製造業、金屬製品業等；(3)1-9 月房地
產開發投資額年減幅由 19.9%擴大至 20.6%、施工面積年減幅為 12.8%，新建商
品房銷售面積年減幅擴大為 13.2%，房市投資、買氣皆持續低迷。 
受到出口維持高度成長帶動，9 月工業生產年增率較 8 月增加 0.3 個百分點
至 5.5%(累計 1-9 月工業生產年增率持平 1-8 月水準為 5.3%)。依產業別來看，9
月主要受到煤礦開採、化工、化纖等產業擴張影響，主因中秋與國慶長假前發電
業充實庫存水位，促使煤炭企業加快復產，加上油價上漲帶動相關中下游供應鏈
增產；惟高科技產品包含 IC、機器人、新能源車等年增率皆有放慢，反映去年中
秋國慶連假皆落在 10 月，使得去年 9 月工作日較多，致基期相對墊高的影響。 
展望未來 ， 儘管政府10 月初宣布釋放 5,500 億元人民幣的地方政府債務結存
限額，但額度下達到資金撥付往往需要數月時間，預期對於第四季的經濟影響有
限，使今年全年經濟成長率預估將落在 4.5%附近。此外，受歐盟右派勢力崛起及
保護主義影響，歐盟對中國貿易政策可能持續趨嚴，後續仍須關注雙方談判及溝
通結果，未來若外需動能減弱，預期中國政府將持續透過發展特定高科技產業及
扶植內需來支持經濟成長動能，以減輕房市不振及貿易環境惡化的衝擊。

五、印度及東南亞—新、馬出口保持強勁增速，菲律賓央行大舉升息 
(1)印度: 10 月 HSBC 綜合採購經理人指數（PMI）初值上升 0.8 點至 56.7，其中，
製造業 PMI 略下滑 0.2 點至 54.9、服務業 PMI 則上揚 1.0 點至 56.2，皆保持穩健
擴張態勢。由於美國、英國、中東及孟加拉等地的訂單增加，帶動新訂單及出口
訂單加速成長，製造商採購庫存增速創八個月來最快、成品庫存增速亦創歷史新
高，然而聘僱指數降至今年以來最低、未完成訂單下降，反映現階段人力充足。
另一方面，投入成本漲幅連續第七個月下降並降至近一年低點，產品售價漲幅亦
明顯放緩，通膨壓力緩解，有助提升企業獲利能力及消費者購買力，並且給予印
度央行（RBI）貨幣政策空間。 
(2)新加坡：9 月非石油國內出口（NODX）年增 68.4%，增速史上最快，連續第
23 個月正成長，其中，人工智慧需求強勁，帶動電子產品出口躍增 107.6%；非電
子產品出口亦大幅成長 54.2%，主要由船舶和遊艇組件所帶動。按出口目的地劃
分 ， 對香港出口激增 121.1%、 對台灣 、 韓國出口增幅達96%， 對美出口成長 73.2%，
但對印度出口下降 5.6%。展望未來，新加坡金融管理局在 10 月的宏觀經濟評估
報告中表示，直至 2027 年人工智慧 (AI) 驅動的成長將保持相對穩定， 支持未
來幾季經濟成長表現。由於基數較高以及人工智慧相關投資預計放緩，全球利率
上升可能會對新加坡主要貿易夥伴的消費和非人工智慧投資造成壓力，可能會影
響外部需求，薪資成長放緩、招聘謹慎以及運輸成本上升可能會在 2026 年下半
年對可自由支配支出和零售活動構成壓力，預計 2027 年 GDP 成長將放緩。 
(3)印尼：印尼央行 （BI）10 月會議連續第四次會議維持基準利率(7 天期逆回購利
率,BI-Rate)於 5.75%不變，貸款便利利率（LFR）維持在 6.50%不變，但將隔夜存
款便利利率（DFR）調高 25 個基點至 5%，旨在提高印尼盾資產的回報率，藉此
吸引並留住外資，同時宣布分兩階段調降存款準備率，11 月 1 日起下調 1 個百分
點至 8%，12 月 1 日起進一步降至 7%，以提供銀行體系流動性。整體而言，印尼
央行爲捍衛印尼盾，在 5 至 6 月間共升息 100 個基點，同時調降外資避險成本，
並採取「資金停留愈久、優惠愈高」的機制吸引外資流入。惟近期國際指數編纂
公司 MSCI 擬將印尼股市由”新興市場”降級為”獨立市場”，引發資金外流疑慮，
央行選擇利率按兵不動，搭配降準提供銀行體系流動性，以兼顧印尼盾穩定與國
內經濟需求，未來 MSCI 檢討進展(最快 2027 年 4 月公布)及印尼盾走勢，將持續
牽動央行後續政策。

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

## Document 114: 261027_daiwa_TPCA

Source PDF: all report pdf/261027_daiwa_TPCA.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-27

### Page 1

Summary: We attended the 2026 Taiwan Printed Circuit Association (TPCA) Show that 
was held on 20-22 October. Below, we share our read-throughs from booth visits and key 
company takeaways. Overall, we remain positive on Taiwan glass fibre, CCL, PCB, and 
substrate vendors, supported by upbeat company commentary. On pricing, we expect 
low-end CCL and E-glass price increases to be larger and more sustained than previously 
anticipated. High-end CCL vendor commentary suggests plans for more meaningful price 
increases from 2H26 to maintain their products’ relative profitability amid sharp increases 
in low-end CCL prices. We also expect prices for T-glass, ABF, CCL, and substrates to 
continue rising. On the CCL technology roadmap, we favour Q-glass combined with 
hydrocarbon resin (HC) over PTFE-based alternatives as the longer-term direction. Lastly, 
we provide our view on the CCL market competition landscape change after seeing 
Nan Ya Plastics targeting the high-end market more aggressively. We also provide some of 
our key highlights from related companies within the Taiwan PCB supply chain. EMC (2383 
TT, TWD4,750, Buy [1]), ITEQ (6213 TT, TWD746, Buy [1]), Kinsus (3189 TT, TWD941, 
Buy [1]) and NYPCB (8046 TT, TWD1,255, Buy [1]) are our top ideas within our coverage.

We attended the 2026 Taiwan Printed Circuit Association (TPCA) Show last week. Below, 
we share our read-throughs from booth visits and key company takeaways.

Whether to adopt PTFE emerged as a key point of debate at the show.  
• During the TPCA show, Taiwan Union Technology (TUC; 6274 TT, TWD1,280, Buy [1]) 
unveiled its new M10 (Hydrocarbon base) and M11 (PTFE base) materials with glass-
fabric (Low-Dk2, Q-glass) and glass-fabric-free versions. Nan Ya Plastic (1303 TT, not 
rated) also unveiled its M11-grade material (ie, NPG-A99) at the show. Based on our 
understanding, NPG-A99 will continue to a hydrocarbon resin (HC) base, likely 
combined with Low-Dk2, to enhance CCL performance for high frequency and high-
speed signal transmission requirements. However, Elite Material (EMC; 2383 TT, 
TWD4,750, Buy [1]) remains reluctant to enter the PTFE-based CCL market, citing 
limited end application. Also, EMC plans to use advanced Q-glass for its M10 CCL. 
Lastly, we also heard from Panasonic’s booth representative that Panasonic (6752 JP, 
JPY3,687, Neutral [3]; covered by Ayaka Inomata) is pursuing a similar strategy for 
M9/M10 development, leveraging Q-glass instead of PTFE at this stage.

• While Nvidia (NVDA US, USD216, Outperform [2]; covered by Louis Miscioscia) 
appears keen to enhance product performance by adopting PTFE, given its very low 
dissipation factor (Df), PCB manufacturing yields remain a key concern. Accordingly, 
some leading vendors are broadening their material options (including PTFE-based 
CCL) to address potential customer requirements. EMC and Panasonic, however, 
remain firmly committed to a Q-glass-based technology roadmap. Although TUC 
highlighted progress on its PTFE-based M11 at a technology conference during the 
show, we believe it also intends to advance its existing HC resin/Low-Dk2 and HC 
resin/Q-glass roadmap for its next-generation CCL. In our view, its PTFE-based M11 
development is intended to preserve flexibility across potential technology pathways 
rather than replace its existing approach. Nan Ya Plastics’s strategy also appears 
reasonable: it can produce Low-Dk glass in-house but must source Q-glass and PTFE 
externally. This sourcing structure may explain its continued use of Low-Dk2 with HC-
resin instead of PTFE for its next-gen products, including M11.

3

 Taiwan PCB: Our key takeaways from 2026 TPCA show

### Page 2

Taiwan Data Centre Hardware 
27 October 2026 
2

• Conclusion: which technology roadmap will prevail? We believe it is too early to 
identify a clear winner, as the supply chain continues to improve manufacturing yields 
for PTFE-based products. Some vendors remain cautious despite PTFE’s long history in 
the industry, as its thermal shrinkage and relative softness still make adoption in high-
layer-count (HLC) PCBs challenging. Given the current stance of leading global vendors 
EMC, Doosan (000150 KS), and Panasonic, together with EMC’s execution track 
record, we currently favour Q-glass-based solutions without PTFE as the more likely 
direction. Nevertheless, broadening product offerings to address different customer 
requirements should not be viewed negatively, provided vendors have sufficient 
resources to support development across multiple technology pathways.

Low-end CCL and e-glass continue to surprise on the upside.  
• During the show, we also spoke with Kingboard Laminates (1888 HK, not rated). The 
company remained positive on the E-glass outlook, although its booth representative 
provided no numerical guidance for next year following a c.200% price hike in 2026. 
Kingboard attributed the pricing strength to supply constraints, driven largely by AI 
server demand. While we highlighted increased glass yarn supply from Chinese peers in 
2Q/3Q26, the company argued that the weaving machine capacity remains the 
bottleneck. It expects to add only c.500 weaving machines in 2026 and 1.2k in 2027 (vs. 
current installed base of 3.3k). E-glass fabric price increases therefore appear 
supported by limited weaving capacity and producers shifting capacity from lower-end 
glass to higher-end products, including Low-Dk, Low-Dk2, and T-glass. Kingboard also 
noted a similar trend in low-end copper foil, where shortages have recently intensified.

• The read-through from Nan Ya Plastics is similar. The company indicated that its current 
fibreglass allocation between internal consumption and external sales is 45:55, with the 
internal share set to rise gradually as it upgrades its product mix. Its CCL portfolio 
remains concentrated in FR-4 (M2 grade), but it plans to migrate toward M4/M6, 
supported by long-term contract wins with two major hyperscalers seeking to secure 
CCL supply. In 2027, M4/M6 products are expected to account for 60-70% of its mix (4m 
out of 6.5m in capacity), with M2 and below contributing 20%. The remainder would 
comprise high-end CCL, including M7/M8/M9, reflecting recent development progress. 
We therefore expect Nan Ya’s external thin-type E-glass supply to decline as its M4/M6 
mix increases next year.

Competition from Nan Ya Plastics.   
• The current CCL shortage creates favourable conditions for Nan Ya Plastics to expand 
into the high-end CCL supply chain. Whether the company has entered AMZN’s T3/T4 
supply chain is also a key point of market debate. Based on discussions with its booth 
representatives, however, we believe management will prioritise upgrading its M4/M6 
product portfolio before pursuing the highest-end CCL market more aggressively. On the 
positive side, favourable supply-demand dynamics have supported Nan Ya’s transition 
from a low-end supplier toward the mid-to-high-end segment, although high-end CCL 
should still contribute a relatively small share of revenue in 2027. For incumbent 
suppliers, competition is clearly intensifying, but we expect the near-term impact on 
high-end peers to remain limited.

• Our read: We expect the impact on EMC and TUC to remain relatively limited in 2027. 
Competitive pressure could become more meaningful over 2028-2030, although it is too 
early to assess its magnitude.

### Page 3

Taiwan Data Centre Hardware 
27 October 2026 
3

Other key takeaways: 
• Nitto Boseki (3110 JP, JPY2,942, Neutral [3]; covered by Noritsugu Hirakawa) identified 
Taiwan Glass (1802 TT, NR) and Grace Fabric Technology (603256 CH, CNY84.95, 
Outperform [2]; covered by Dennis Ip) as key competitors. Its plan to triple capacity by 
2028 remains largely on track. The company also intends to expand capacity further by 
2030, although the details have yet to be confirmed.

• Resonac (4004 JP, JPY12,915, Outperform [2]; covered by Hidemitsu Umebayashi) 
indicated that customers are requesting ABF substrate core thicknesses of 2,000-
3,000μm, versus its current maximum of 1,600μm, to accommodate larger package 
sizes. Potential technology pathways include adopting glass cores or ceramic 
substrates, or stacking additional core layers to enhance rigidity. Based on discussions 
with booth representatives, substrate makers may move toward multi-core structures, 
which could increase T-glass consumption. However, technical challenges in enabling 
signal transmission between core layers remain a potential barrier that would need to be 
addressed over the next one to two years.

• Mitsui Kinzoku (5706 JP, JPY2,054, Outperform [2]; covered by Shinichiro Ozaki) 
indicated that, based on its technology roadmap, M10-grade CCL combined with its 
HVLP5 copper foil should support 448Gbps SerDes transmission. In the near term, the 
company plans to shift capacity from lower-end to higher-end HVLP products, with a key 
piece of production equipment scheduled for installation in 2028/29. On pricing, its booth 
representative suggested that quarterly increases of more than 10% would be 
appropriate. Regarding copper foil shortages highlighted by other supply-chain 
participants, the representative indicated that mid-tier products, such as HVLP1 and 
HVLP2, could face the most severe supply constraints.

• Unimicron (3037 TT, TWD1,060, Buy [1]) appeared to highlight multi-core structures and 
the embedding of passive components within core layers as key technology trends in 
ABF substrates.

• Zhen Ding Technology (ZDT; 4958 TT, not rated) indicated that its substrate business 
has a plan to list on the Hong Kong Stock Exchange in 1H27 under the name Leading 
Interconnect Semiconductor Technology (LIST). At the show, the company focused 
primarily on thick HDI boards and highlighted two next-generation technology trends: 1) 
sequential lamination (M+N) to improve signal integrity and increase routing density 
within the same footprint; and 2) hybrid HDI/HLC structures, which add HDI layers to 
thick HLC boards (M+N), as discussed earlier.

UMC  | Asia Pacific
  Near term in line but larger 
long-term AI opportunity; stay

Larger AI opportunity into 2029: The company raised 2029 AI-related revenue 
guidance from >US$1bn to 25% of total revenue. Assuming ~15% YoY growth on our 
2028e revenue (c. US$14bn), 2029 pro forma AI revenue could reach ~US$4bn (25% 
of ~US$16bn) –  well above the prior US$1bn. Also, newly announced 12-inch silicon 
photonics capacity of 18kwpm could generate at least US$1.7–1.8bn assuming ~US
$8k wafer ASP – not to mention newly announced capacity of another 22kwpm for 
DTC/silicon capacitors and 20kwpm for Teflon modulators.
2027 price hike plan in line with our estimate: The company guided for a 10% 
improvement in 2027 blended wafer ASP. Excluding better product mix, like-for-like 
wafer ASP would rise 5–10%, which is in line with our estimate but slightly below 
the buy-side bogey of 10-15%. 
Weak consumer could be covered by other non-AI demand: UMC does  see some 
weakness showing up in PCs and midrange/low-end smartphones, but 
communications,  automotive, and industrial demand remains robust.

## Document 171: 261101_ms_nuvoton

Source PDF: all report pdf/261101_ms_nuvoton.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-01
Pages: 13
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

M
Update
Nuvoton Technology Corporation  | Asia Pacific
Losses Widen; BMC Growth and
Japan Restructuring Remain Key
3Q losses widened Q/Q amid lower gross profit, increased opex
and reduced non-operating income. Management sees scope for
faster BMC growth in 2027, with execution and Japan
restructuring critical to delivering earnings growth.
3Q losses widened: Revenue was NT$7.86bn, -1% Q/Q, +8% Y/Y, 3% above our
estimate; gross profit was NT$2.9bn, -3% Q/Q, +13% Y/Y, 6% above our forecast;

gross margin was 37.4%, -0.6ppt Q/Q, +1.7ppt Y/Y, 1.2ppt above our estimate. Loss
per share was NT$1.24, wider Q/Q, Y/Y, and vs. our expectation. Management
attributed gross margin weakness to product mix; back-end supply constraints and
substantial AI-related R&D left operational progress around 1Q behind plan.
Demand remains mixed: Auto demand has stabilized after prior Y/Y declines, but
management sees no clear rebound. PC demand was significantly weaker in 2H26

than in 1H26 with higher content per PC vs. 2025 helping cushion the impact of
lower volumes. Memory cost increases have been passed through on memory-
bundled MPUs, although some customers began pulling back in 4Q26. MCU
repricing reflects foundry and OSAT costs. Most 2027 capacity has been secured but
cost pass-through and supply availability do not establish margin expansion.
BMC and Japan remain the key milestones: Management sees scope for 2027 BMC

Y/Y growth to exceed 2026's, with existing customers and multiple new customers,
including a CSP, contributing. New customer revenue should initially be small with
fuller contributions over one to two years. Current-gen products should dominate
2027 shipments; next-gen volumes may start modestly in 2H27. Japan remains the
source of losses, making the Tower JV restructuring targeted by management for
end-March 2027 central to recovery.
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+

Ethan Jia
Research Associate

recovery before turning more positive on
the stock, which we view as overvalued.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

VPEC (2455.TW): Optoelectronics mix upgrade driving GM expansion; 
3Q26 in line; Buy
2 November 2026 | 5:30AM HKT
3Q26 revenues increased 39% QoQ, with optoelectronics (mainly InP epiwafer for PD 
and CW lasers) revenues contribution up to 43% (vs. 27% in 2Q26), driving GM up to 
45% (vs. 39% in 2Q26). Along with opex ratio further improved to 13.7% (vs. 15.4% 
in 2Q26), VPEC’s OP income increased 87% QoQ in 3Q26, or 2% behind / 7% above 
our / Bloomberg consensus estimates. Looking ahead, we expect 4Q26E revenues to 
further increase by 14% QoQ, with GM up to 46% driven by product mix upgrade 
toward Optoelectronics. The company also announced its issuance of unsecured 
convertible bonds valued at NT$1.5bn (US$46m), to supplement its procurement in 
raw materials and equipment, reflecting the strong demand and VPEC’s commitment 
in capacity expansion; as we highlighted in Oct (Global Server TAM updates) where 
our US Internet team forecast that leading US cloud service providers (CSPs) in 
aggregate would grow their capex budgets by 84%/ 52%/ 13% YoY in 2026E-28E 
and where we raise our estimates by 4% / 17% / 22% in 2026-28E to US$1,397bn by 
2028E. We remain positive view on VPEC, which we believe is positioned to benefit 
from the AI server racks ramp up, optical networking specification upgrades across 
transmitters / CW lasers (toward 1.6T optical modules) and receivers (toward 200G 
PD), product expansion toward EML base epiwafers, further customer penetration 
for CW lasers, improving InP substrate supply, and opportunities in InP epiwafers for 
high power lasers for NPO and CPO. Maintain Buy.

3-month revenue preview: VPEC delivered solid revenue growth at 39% QoQ / 47% 
YoY in 3Q26; Looking ahead, we expect +14% QoQ in 4Q26E, riding on new AI server 
racks ramp up and optical networking specification upgrade toward 1.6T optical

Earnings revision: We factor in 3Q26 results and cut our net income by 7% in 2026E, to 
reflect modest revenue moderation from smartphone RF pacing alongside a higher R&D 
expense ratio for AI data center products. Despite the cut, we continue to expect 
sequentially higher revenues in 4Q26E with GM continuing to expand in 4Q26E vs. 3Q26, 
driven by new AI server racks ramp up and optical networking specification upgrade 
toward 1.6T optical modules, along with capacity expansion and better InP substrate 
supply to better capture the demand. We keep our 2027E-28E estimates largely 
unchanged.

Valuation: We continue to derive our 12m target price from 2029E P/E discounted back 
to 2027E at 10.5% COE (no change), and derive our target P/E multiple from refreshed 
peers’ trading P/E vs. forward year fundamentals (NI YoY and OPM). Based on our 
2029E-30E NI YoY of 18% (unchanged) and 2029-30E OPM of 38% (unchanged), and 
peers’ PEG&M of 0.4x (unchanged), we derive target P/E multiple at 22.0x (vs. 22.2x 
previously), reflecting a slight peer group multiple compression amid the product 
transition window from 800G to 1.6T optical transceivers, balanced by sustained 
long-term AI optical networking demand. Our new 12-month target price is at NT$785 
(vs. NT$793 previously). Our new TP implies 2027E P/E at 68.7x (vs. 69.4x previously), 
within the company’s trading range (vs. peak at 74x, and +1stv. at 47x). Maintain Buy.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that

주가 추이

견조한 판매 확대로 매출 견조, 이익은 시장 기대치 하회 예상 
3Q26 동사 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 33.6조원(+17.3% 이하 Y oY)과 2.39조
원(+63.6%) 기록 전망. 3Q 매출액은 친환경차 중심 신차 확대 통해 두 자릿수 
성장세를 기록. 3Q 판매량은 도매 기준 84.3만대(+7.6% 이하 Y oY)로 전년 동
기 대비 성장 폭을 확대했고, 특히 친환경차 판매량 29.8만대(+45.9%)를 기록
하며 이번 분기 매출 성장에 절대적으로 기여.  
다만, 3Q 수익성은 4~5월 상승했던 원자재 투입 영향과 ‘경제형EV 믹스’가 더
욱 확대(13.2% → 14.5%)되면서 단기 수익성에 부정적 작용하며 시장 기대치 
2.5조원을 다소 하회할 전망. 그러나, 기말 환율이 1,356원/$ 수준으로 낮 아 지
면서 전년 동기 수 익 성 악화에 크게 영 향 을 미쳤던 판매보증비 외화환산손익 
영향이 개선(1.6%p 수준) 기대되며, 관세 부담 또한 북미 HEV 확대로 전년 동
기 대비 약 4,500억원(2.0%p) 감소하면서 부정적 수익성 영향 만회 전망.  
유럽은 점유율 확대, 북미는 수익성 개선으로 
올해 북미 HEV와 유럽 BEV 중심 신차 출시 및 물량 확대가 본격화되면
서, ’27년에는 이익 성장 기여도가 확대될 것으로 전망. 미국 조 지 아 텔 루 라 이
드 HEV 신규 생산(2.8만대) 및 HMGMA 스 포 티 지HEV신규 생산(1.0만
대) 등 수 요 가 높은 북미 현 지 에 서 HEV 물량 확대 중으로 4Q에도 텔루라이
드 HEV와 스포티지 HEV의 현지 생산/판매 확대를 비롯하여 셀토스 HEV 
출시로 ’27년 북미 중심 HEV 신차 물량 증가 및 믹스 개선 기대. 유럽은 강한 
BEV 수요가 지속되면서 중국 업체들의 공세 강화 중이나 동사의 물량 확대 또
한 견조. 3Q EV3 2.9만대, EV5 2.2만대 등 유럽 BEV 수요 대응 전략 주효. ’27
년에는 K4/셀토스 및 쏘렌토 등 HEV 신차 중 심 의 친환경차 PT 믹스 다양화
로 유럽 내 물량 확대 흐름 지속 전망 
물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결, 기말 배당 매력도 부각 
’27년 동사 영업이익 추 정 치 는 11.1조원(+13.5%)으로 증익 지속 전망. 최근 
환율 추세 고려 시 내년 수익성에 부 정 적 영향 확대 가능성 존재하나, 올해 확
인된 신차 중심 물량 확대를 통해 부정적 환율 영향을 상쇄할 것 으 로 기대. 
12M EPS 추정치 하향(-2.9%) 및 상 반 기 적 용 한 코스피 프리미엄 제거(-
10%)로 목 표 주 가 를 260,000원으로 하향. 그러나, RA 설립에서의 동사 지분 
참여 및 기말 배당(’26년 DPS 7,000원 추정)에 따른 상 대 적 배당 수익 매력
도 부각 등은 주가 모 멘 텀 에 있어 기대 가능한 요소.

저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

## Document 309: Business Checkpoint_ Looking into the logistics Profitability growth was positive in the third quarter, but logistics costs and supply chain pressures are creating challenges. 2 mi

Source PDF: all report pdf/Business Checkpoint_ Looking into the logistics Profitability growth was positive in the third quarter, but logistics costs and supply chain pressures are creating challenges. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13028181     1

Economy 
Business Checkpoint: Looking into the logistics 
15 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Small and mid-sized businesses entered Q3 in relatively solid financial condition, with profitability growth up across both 
groups, according to Bank of America business account data. Nearly half of estimated tariff payments had been refunded by 
September, though the share of these refunds relative to revenue has peaked. 
• In Bank of America business payments data, logistics spending growth per client was up four times as much for small than mid-
sized firms. Additionally, distribution and inventory transactions growth per client has outpaced spending since the spring of 
2025. 
• Growing transportation bottlenecks are re-emerging ahead of the holiday season. Trucking capacity has tightened as driver 
availability remains constrained, according to BofA Global Research. This has pushed freight rates higher and driven strong 
transportation sector payroll growth, particularly among mid-sized businesses, according to Bank of America payments data.

Business Checkpoint is a new, regular publication from Bank of America Institute. It provides a real-time view of the financial health, opportunities 
and challenges facing small and mid-sized businesses across the US economy, leveraging the depth and breadth of Bank of America's proprietary 
data. By analyzing activity among both small and mid-sized firms, Business Checkpoint offers timely insights into business profitability, hiring, 
investment and spending trends. Such data is not intended to be reflective or indicative of, and should not be relied upon as, the results of operations, 
financial condition or performance of Bank of America. 
Businesses end Q3 in fair shape 
While profitability growth remained positive for both small and mid-sized firms in the third quarter, small firms outpaced their 
mid-sized counterparts for the second consecutive quarter (Exhibit 2). Still, there’s been a notable reversal in the profitability of 
mid-sized businesses, growing this quarter after declining slightly for the past two.  
The continued rollout of tariff refunds is serving as a tailwind to some firms' cash positions. That is particularly true for small 
firms. According to Bank of America account data, by September approximately 47% of estimated International Emergency 
Economic Powers Act-related tariff payments had been refunded to companies with less than $50 million in annual revenue.

### Page 2

2 15 October 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

According to Bank of America business payments data, the percentage point difference in tariff refunds as a share of revenue 
for firms with less than $50 million in annual revenue spiked in June but dropped to nearly zero in September. At the same time, 
the percentage point difference in tariff payments as a share of expenses began rising again relative to the 2023 average 
(Exhibit 1). This suggests that while tariffs continue to affect companies, the refund program has provided temporary relief for 
some businesses.  
Wholesale trade along with transportation and warehousing posted solid profitability growth across business size 
However, the benefits appear uneven. Such refunds have boosted cash flow, but they have not fully insulated firms from broader 
cost pressures.  
Looking across industries, mid-sized construction and wholesale firms had the strongest profitability growth in September 
(Exhibit 3). Notably, transportation and warehousing, as well as wholesale trade were among the few sectors showing positive 
profitability growth for both small and mid-sized firms last month. 
The persistence of logistics, freight and inventory-related cost pressures may help explain why profitability gains have remained 
relatively concentrated in transportation and wholesale trade. However, these sectors also have a higher exposure to rising gas 
prices within their margins (read more on this in the August Small Business Checkpoint). 
Exhibit 3: For both small and mid-sized businesses, profitability growth was positive in transportation and warehousing as well as wholesale trade 
in September 
Profitability growth by sector and business size in September (YoY%, six-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The cost of maintaining supply chains is rising 
In fact, the prices paid component of the September services reading from the Institute for Supply Management indicates still-
building price pressure for firms (Exhibit 4). This marked the highest reading since July 2022.   
Using Bank of America internal data, we identified payments to logistics and freight companies to assess a key part of supply 
chain costs. In June, logistics and freight payments’ growth per client from small businesses grew nearly 30%, though has 
moderated to 12.7% YoY in September (Exhibit 5). Meanwhile, mid-sized firms’ payments growth in this category was up just 
3.2% YoY in September. 
The divergence suggests logistics costs may be disproportionately affecting smaller firms, who are typically less capable of 
absorbing rising costs than larger firms. Larger companies also often benefit from dedicated logistics contracts and greater 
purchasing power, while smaller ones may be more exposed to spot-market transportation costs and fluctuations in freight 
pricing.

Evidence of more moving parts in companies’ supply chains 
Yet there is increasing evidence that tariffs and geopolitics are adding complexity to small and mid-sized companies’ supply 
chains. 
In Bank of America payments data, average distribution and inventory transaction growth per business client has increased 
steadily since the spring of 2025 despite spending growth in this category falling in September (Exhibit 6). Rising transaction 
growth alongside softer spending growth suggests businesses may be engaging with more suppliers, shipments or inventory 
movements even as overall procurement outlays moderates.  
Furthermore, in May 2026, the Global Supply Chain Pressure Index reached its highest reading since the summer of 2022 and has 
continued to remain elevated into October (Exhibit 7). This further emphasizes that transportation bottlenecks, delays and 
procurement challenges are becoming more common globally. While current readings remain below the extreme levels observed 
during the pandemic, the recent move higher indicates supply chain normalization may be reversing.

per business client has increased steadily since the spring of 2025

The trials of trucking  
As of October 1, BofA Global Research's proprietary bi-weekly Truckload Demand Indicator (see Methodology) remained below 
its historical growth signal (Exhibit 9) and the Truck Capacity Indicator, which reflects shippers' views of available capacity, 
tightened significantly, falling from 39.2 to 28.4 (Exhibit 8). This suggests shippers are seeing fewer trucks available relative to 
demand than in the previous survey. 
According to BofA Global Research, the current trucking upcycle is being driven by constrained supply rather than a significant 
improvement in freight demand. While any positive movement in demand is an important indicator as the industry moves into 
peak shipping season between late September and early December, tighter capacity appears to be the dominant force 
supporting freight rates. 
Exhibit 8: Shippers’ view of available capacity has fallen 
significantly as of October 1, 2026 
BofA Truckload Capacity Indicator by year (bi-weekly)

months after 4.5 months above 
BofA Truckload Demand Indicator (bi-weekly)

Source: BofA Global Research  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The reduction in capacity reflects several factors, including stricter commercial driver's license (CDL) and training requirements, 
increased compliance and enforcement measures, and other regulatory changes that have reduced the pool of qualified drivers. 
This has also likely driven some wage inflation within the industry. According to Bank of America payments data, transportation 
and warehousing payroll growth per client has surged since the start of the year, especially for mid-sized businesses (Exhibit 10).  
Having to pay higher wages suggests labor shortages remain a challenge in the sector. Firms may increasingly need to offer 
higher compensation to attract and retain workers as trucking capacity tightens. In turn, such capacity puts upward pressure on 
freight rates, particularly in the spot market where small businesses may be more impacted.

Logistics payments tend to increase in the second half of the year 
September marks a key run-up to the holiday season (read more on signs of holiday spending in the October Consumer 
Checkpoint), with spending on ocean shipping, containers, and other transportation services steadily accelerating ahead of the 
peak shopping period.  
In fact, over the past two years, the month of November has seen ocean shipping/container spending volumes well above their 
2023-2025 monthly average (Exhibit 11). The seasonal pattern likely reflects inventory restocking and holiday-related shipping 
activity. Ocean shipping volumes typically accelerate during the fall months as businesses prepare for year-end demand. 
Exhibit 11: Ocean shipping/container spending volumes were well above their 2023-2025 monthly average in November 
Logistics and freight average payments by category (monthly, indexed, 100 = monthly 2023-2025 average)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Distributors and inventory payments’ growth fell ahead of holiday season 
Changes in logistics and freight spending often provide an early signal of inventory trends, as distributors account for a large 
share of supply chain-related spending (Exhibit 12). 
The sharp moderation in distribution and inventory spending growth per client for both small and mid-sized businesses indicates 
that much of the large-scale inventory accumulation seen earlier in 2025 has already occurred (Exhibit 13). In other words, 
businesses appear to be replenishing inventories for seasonal demand rather than significantly expanding stockpiles as they did 
earlier in the year. 
Overall, businesses seem to be entering the holiday season in relatively stable financial condition, supported by tariff refunds 
and positive profitability growth. However, rising logistics costs, tighter trucking capacity and elevated supply chain pressures 
suggest operating conditions are becoming more challenging.

Methodology 
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business and/or Business Banking clients with a deposit account or a 
Small Business credit card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of 
America per Business client data represents activity spending from active Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card and at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and are 
generally defined as under $5mm in annual sales revenue. Mid-sized businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America 
and are generally defined as $5mm-$50mm in annual sales revenue. 
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
Revenue tiers are determined by the combination of following factors: 1) stated revenue on small businesses credit applications, 2) actual 
account inflow into Bank of America Deposit Accounts, and 3) third party revenue estimation.  
The alternative hiring indicator consists of payments from Bank of America business clients to business-focused hiring firms which include both 
direct deposits through Automated Clearing House (ACH) and payments via credit and debit cards. 
Supply chain-related spending is comprised of payments to logistics, freight, distribution, inventory, delivery service, and trucking companies. 
These are identified from merchant names and MCC codes. 
BofA Global Research’s trucking indicators are a bi-weekly diffusion-style survey of shipper demand expectations over the next 0–3 months, 
built from responses from surveyed shippers. 
Data regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

## Document 331: COP31 Preview_ Cooperation Amid Fragmentation (2026-10-30)

Source PDF: all report pdf/COP31 Preview_ Cooperation Amid Fragmentation (2026-10-30).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 22
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

COP31 Preview: Cooperation Amid Fragmentation
COP31, taking place in Antalya, Türkiye from 9 to 20 November 2026, will test whether the ‘Implementation COP’ can
turn climate ambition into finance delivery amid heightened geopolitical tensions. Corporates and investors should
watch for signals that could shape policy and capital allocation, including commitments to grid investment,
electrification, adaptation and carbon markets. We also expect AI’s climate potential and rising electricity demand to

Deutsche Bank
Research
Debbie Jones | Andrew Dixon
S U S T A I N A B I L I T Y  | T H E M A T I C  R E S E A R C H  | O C T O B E R  2 0 2 6
The report outlines the top 10 focus topics, discussions and outcomes for COP31 (see pages 1-3 for a summary)

### Page 2

C O P 3 1  P r ev iew  |  TO P  1 0  (1  o f 3 )
Antalya  - 10 things to watch for at COP31
2 Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Preview  |  October 2026
From 9 to 20 November, ~100,000 participants and nearly 200 Parties are expected at COP31 in Antalya, Türkiye. This is an ‘Implementation COP’ and will focus on climate finance delivery for
mitigation and adaptation commitments. Yes, there will be geopolitical tensions and competitiveness concerns. But, for corporates and investors, the discussions will shape how private capital is

mobilised, and how policies will affect de-risking efforts. We expect electrification efforts will feature prominently, driven by energy security concerns as well as by rising AI-driven power
demand. Furthermore, AI’s ability to help tackle climate change has the potential to play a bigger role in shaping the agenda of future COPs.
01
TÜRKIYE HOSTS, AUSTRALIA LEADS
NEGOTIATIONS
Türkiye and Australia must stay aligned
to drive progress
See page 6
– As host, Türkiye’s Murat Kurum will preside over COP31 and its Action Agenda, which is intended to build cooperation between

willing participants to drive climate action. Australia’s Chris Bowen will run the formal negotiations that will seek consensus (or
lack of objection) on finance delivery and existing climate commitments. Feedback following pre-COP meetings on the
arrangement has been broadly positive, but concern remains over how any potential disagreement will be handled.
– This structure is the result of a compromise after both countries bid to host. Both countries want COP31 to focus on climate

finance implementation and clean-energy growth, however, they remain coal-reliant (in different ways) with no national coal
phase-out date, a criticism likely to continue from climate advocates at COP.
02
CLIMATE FINANCE
Success at COP31 requires notable
progress on mobilising existing
commitments
See page 7
– For COP31 to be successful it will need to deliver agreements to mobilise and channel climate finance. For context, at COP29 in
2024 it was agreed (as part of formal negotiations) that developed nations would commit $300bn in climate finance annually

by 2035, with a call for $1.3tn from all sources. At present, more credible plans are needed to secure existing commitments
and attract broader capital. Note the EU has already stated it will not make new finance pledges at COP31.
– Article 9 contains the main financing provisions of the Paris Agreement and has driven much debate during COP talks.
Developing countries want focus on public funding from developed governments (Article 9.1), while developed countries prefer

to discuss Article 9 in a broader context. This allows private capital to be included. COP30 launched a two-year work
programme on Article 9, but June’s Bonn talks stalled, so COP31 meetings will attempt to reach agreement.
03
ADAPTATION & RESILIENCE
Implications of a 1.5°C overshoot point
to more adaptation financing
See pages 8–9
– The UN has warned that an overshoot of the Paris Agreement’s 1.5°C goal is now unavoidable, at least temporarily. That has

dampened morale for climate supporters ahead of COP but, at the same time, it has drawn more attention to the topic of
adaptation finance, which is set to be on the formal negotiating agenda. Parties appear deadlocked over whether the COP30
goal to triple adaptation finance by 2035 (potential annual target of $120bn) should be explicitly referenced in the final COP31
decision on the Global Goal on Adaptation (GGA). This is important, as anchoring it to the GGA could signal finance flows.

– The EU Commission proposed the Climate Resilience law on 27 October. It needs Parliament and Council approval, but if
approved, member states will be required to run national climate-risk assessments based on a 3°C-by-2100 warming scenario
(i.e. the EU is planning for overshoot). Members would also need to adopt resilience plans, including details of costs and
funding, with The Commission estimating the EU needs €70bn a year for adaptation to 2050, vs. €29bn of current spend.

### Page 3

Antalya  - 10 things to watch for at COP31
3 Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Preview  |  October 2026
04
ELECTRIFICATION
Türkiye to champion voluntary
electrification commitments
See page 10
– Türkiye's voluntary Action Agenda includes a ‘35-by-35’ target to lift electricity’s share of final energy use from ~23% to 35% by
2035, alongside waste, building energy intensity and circularity targets. This sits outside the formal negotiations and commits
only those that volunteer. China and the EU have voiced support, and the timing is right given global energy-security concerns,

though some question the emissions impact if fossil fuels are used to generate the additional electricity.
06
TRADE TENSIONS
Trade’s inclusion in the provisional
agenda is contentious, with the
potential for disagreement over trade to
stall progress on wider agreements
See page 12
– The role of trade at COP is contentious, but China wants it discussed. On 21 October, Vice Minister Li Gao called ‘unilateralism
and protectionism’ a ‘new problem’ undermining collective climate efforts. Specifically, he wants COP31 to discuss the free

flow of green technology. This was widely read as being aimed at US and EU tariffs on Chinese cleantech.
– Despite a COP30 agreement to create three annual trade dialogues in 2026–28 (the first formal space under the UNFCCC to
discuss trade) away from COP, developing countries are still pushing for trade to be a formal negotiating item in Antalya, and it
is on the provisional agenda. They argue that climate-related trade measures such as the CBAM burden their exporters and

question whether they are compatible with UNFCCC principles. The EU meanwhile claims they help prevent carbon leakage,
with developed countries preferring trade discussions to focus on technical information-sharing without duplicating the WTO.
C O P 3 1  P r ev iew  |  TO P  1 0  (2  o f 3 )
07
GEOPOLITICS & MULTILATERALISM
Consensus under strain, but ‘coalitions
of the willing’ are rising
See pages 13–14
– Geopolitical fragmentation and the US exit from the Paris Agreement make consensus, historically central to COP decision-

making, harder among nearly 200 nations. This has encouraged more voluntary action and “coalitions of the willing” to pursue
more ambitious goals. If these strategies deliver more effective progress, their approach will shape future COPs.
05
FOSSIL FUELS
Phase-out likely not on the formal
agenda, but workarounds are emerging
See page 11
– Brazil’s COP30 presidency is to present a fossil fuel transition roadmap separately, outside the UN formal negotiating process.

For perspective, COP28 in Dubai reached consensus on “transitioning away from fossil fuels in energy systems”, but at COP30
in Belém (2025) calls from more than 80 countries for a transition roadmap faced opposition from other nations, so it was
omitted from the final decision and moved to a separate track.
– Santa Marta is a second workaround for the lack of formal agreement on a transition roadmap. The Colombia- and
Netherlands-led process is a ‘coalition of the willing’ of 57 participants (excluding the US, China, India and Russia) agreeing to

voluntary action to move away from fossil fuels faster than consensus allows. At COP31, Colombia and the Netherlands want
to bring the Santa Marta outcomes into the summit. The format is not yet settled, but the Australian and Turkish presidencies
have said they are open to it. The process then heads to a second conference in Tuvalu, co-hosted with Ireland, in 2027.

### Page 4

09
NATURE & CARBON CREDITS
Efforts to boost demand and build
credibility to help scale carbon markets
See page 16
– Carbon credit discussions will centre on government policies to boost demand and corporate engagement, with much of the
formal UN architecture now in place. If carbon markets can scale, the benefits would be wide-ranging (e.g. supporting
emissions targets and providing finance for developing countries). Credibility and greenwashing concerns remain key risks,

however; we expect prices to diverge in favour of high-integrity credits.
– The Tropical Forest Forever Facility (TFFF), launched at COP30, plans a fixed-income portfolio to reward verified forest
conservation. It now has ~$7.8bn of sponsor capital against a $10bn end-2026 target. In our view, fresh COP31 commitments
would signal appetite to fund nature conservation and protection.
10
PACIFIC, OCEANS & SEA-LEVEL RISE
Australia to bring Pacific priorities into

focus
See page 17
– Australia asked for a Pacific pre-COP in return for ceding the hosting. It has promised to keep championing Pacific interests at
COP31. The Taku Pakasoa Declaration was adopted at that pre-COP on 6 October. ‘Taku Pakasoa’ is Tuvaluan for strength
found in unity and collective action. It is a joint call to keep 1.5°C alive, calling for action on climate finance, resilience and
oceans. Its intention is to give the Pacific a shared position going into COP31 and signal its policy priorities to other blocs.

Other countries are invited to sign on. The climate-ocean nexus will also be a COP31 focus, including investable solutions.
COP31 should indicate what COP can deliver in a more fragmented geopolitical world. Key questions? Can the current structure help mobilise meaningful finance and investment, and do
smaller coalitions and voluntary commitments deliver meaningful outcomes? Answers here could shape future COP agendas.  Looking ahead, Ethiopia will host COP32 in 2027. There, we

expect a continued focus on delivering on commitments and improving access to climate finance, reflecting African priorities. COP33 then falls to Asia-Pacific in 2028, with China
reportedly considering a bid and South Korea potentially interested.
We should note that the second Global Stocktake, the Paris Agreement’s five-yearly review of collective progress, begins at COP31 and concludes at COP33, with a technical assessment
(2027) informing the final negotiations. Its outcome should inform countries’ next national climate plans (NDCs, their emissions-reduction pledges), due in 2030.

08
AI & CLIMATE
AI’s power demand in focus, with the
Antalya pledge calling for low emissions
infrastructure
See page 15
– The Antalya AI Pledge invites governments and companies to commit voluntarily to sustainable infrastructure and
environmental transparency. Its call for zero- and low-emissions energy infrastructure leaves room for nuclear power,
prompting discussion of its role in meeting data-centre demand alongside renewables. While we see binding commitments as

unlikely, watch for corporate power commitments and technology partnerships.
– We expect AI’s climate potential and rising electricity demand to feature prominently at COP31, prompting debate over data-
centre energy use. We also expect AI to help delegations manage COP’s heavy paperwork, from reviewing negotiating texts
and submissions to comparing successive draft decisions. This should help negotiating teams prepare more effectively and
make decisions faster, particularly smaller delegations with limited staff and resources.

### Page 6

TO P I C  1 | COP31’s UNIQUE STRUCTURE
COP31’s unique dual structure will need to bridge divides among negotiating groups and build consensus
As host, Türkiye’s Murat Kurum will preside over COP31 and its Action Agenda. Australia’s Chris Bowen will run the formal negotiations. This dual structure is the result of a compromise after
both countries bid to host. Feedback post pre-COP meetings on the unique arrangement has been broadly positive, but concern remains over how any disagreement between the two countries

would be handled should it occur. Both countries clearly want this year’s COP to focus on climate finance implementation and clean-energy growth.
A unique leadership structure
COP31 will take place in Antalya, Türkiye. The setup is unusual, emerging as part of a deal
between Türkiye and Australia after both countries bid to host the event. What began as a
compromise could become an advantage, provided the two countries remain aligned. Their
differing diplomatic relationships could help them bridge divides between negotiating groups

and build common ground on climate action and finance.
How it will work
Murat Kurum, Türkiye’s Minister of the Environment, will serve as COP31 President, overseeing
the conference and Action Agenda. Australia’s Minister for Climate Change and Energy, Chris
Bowen, will serve as President of Negotiations, including drafting final texts, with authority to
steer government-to-government talks. Pre-COP preparations have reportedly been positive.
They will, however, both likely face criticism from climate advocates asking for a plan to shift

from fossil fuels, notably coal, given efforts to position themselves as global climate leaders.
The Action Agenda includes targets for electrification, waste, buildings and circularity
This voluntary Agenda proposes 2035 targets of a 35% electricity share of final energy use (up
from 23% currently), halving projected municipal waste growth, cutting building energy
intensity by at least 25% and reaching at least 15% circular material use. While pledges are

non-binding, they can signal policy and decisions that could support infrastructure investment.
Formal negotiations to focus on implementing existing commitments
Formal negotiations seek consensus (or lack of objection) on implementation pathways. The
key need is guidance on how agreed climate-finance commitments will be funded and
delivered, and on countries’ roles. Adaptation will also be a focus, with countries aiming to
reach agreement on how to advance the Global Goal on Adaptation after discussions stalled

earlier this year. These formal talks remain separate from the voluntary Action Agenda.
Sources: COP31 Action Agenda, IEA and Eco-Business
6

### Page 7

TO P I C  2 | C L I MATE F I N AN C E
Climate finance delivery will be a central test for COP31
June’s Bonn preparatory talks reportedly made little progress and exposed persistent finance divisions, leaving COP31 to turn both the $300bn and $1.3tn-by-2035 goals into a more credible
delivery plan. This is a big ask and requires clearer public/private roles, consistent tracking mechanisms, more concessional finance, risk-sharing and guarantees that can help reduce the cost of

capital. New large-scale finance commitments appear unlikely, with the EU already stating it will not make new pledges.
7
Climate finance: needs, commitments and progress
The image below explains the various climate finance estimates and goals.

$1.3 trillion a year by 2035
COP31 focus: first implementation update on the Baku-Belem
roadmap to $1.3T. This total is inclusive of the $300bn NCQG target.
Context: COP29 set the New Collective Quantified Goal (NCQG),
calling on all actors to scale climate finance for developing countries
to at least $1.3tn a year by 2035. No country has an assigned share,

and private capital is expected to count.
At COP31: first implementation update on the Baku-Belem roadmap
to $1.3T, based on “measurable progress” against targets including:
• a global monitoring framework to track progress in climate finance
mobilization, identify bottlenecks, and highlight emerging solutions.
• clear pathways for public, private, domestic and international
finance;
• Expanding operability, comparability, and interoperability of

climate-finance data; and
• action on grants and concessional finance, debt and fiscal space,
private-finance costs, capacity and system reform.
Are the NCQG and IHLEG aligned? IHLEG’s $1.3tn external-finance
estimate (within the $3.2tn needed excl. China) does align with the
NCQG $1.3tn goal and underpins the Baku-to-Belém Roadmap.
However, they are built differently: political target vs needs-based
estimate.
$300 billion a year by 2035
COP31 focus: what counts and who contributes. This amount

contributes towards the $1.3tn NCQG target from all actors.
Context: Developed countries agreed to lead mobilisation of at least
$300bn a year by 2035, counting public finance and the private
finance it mobilises; no country has a fixed share.
At COP31: Focus turns to how to deliver. Developing countries are
pushing for the development of action plans to chart a pathway to
$300bn and for the Article 9 work programme to be included on the
formal agenda (with emphasis on Article 9.1 – which explicitly covers

developed country finance provision).
Adaptation finance
COP31 focus: embedding and tracking the tripling pledge
Context: COP30 called for adaptation finance to at least triple by
2035, yet this was relatively vague; it did not specify a base year or a
target figure (some have suggested $120bn, i.e. 3x the 2025 goal).
At COP31: Parties need to resolve disagreement on whether to
anchor the pledge in the Global Goal on Adaptation (GGA) and put a
finer point on the agreed Belém Adaptation Indicators that will track

it, after disagreement stalled the June Bonn talks.
Loss and Damage Fund
COP31 focus: guidance, replenishment and access
Why it matters: The Fund is the only dedicated channel for
irreversible climate impacts, yet it has no annual funding target and
resources remain far below demand.
At COP31: Focus will be on its long-term funding strategy, with the
first replenishment cycle set to begin in 2027. $342m made available
in the first funding window, which offers $5–20m grants with at least

half reserved for SIDS and LDCs; but the voluntary nature of
payments, disputes over who pays and sensitivity around
“compensation” make major new pledges in Antalya unlikely.

Hurdles to these
targets
Grants vs loans

Developing countries want grants (especially fo r
adaptation), citing debt burdens and historical
responsibility; funders favour loans and blended
structures that include private capital.
Pressure on development banks
The US is pushing MDBs (e.g. the World Bank), to drop
their climate focus, according to the World Resources
Institute (WRI). Developing countries want MDB reform
to deliver more investment and faster disbursement.
Competing budget pressures

Donors are diverting aid to fund domestic priorities
such as defence. There is also the reality that climate is
now a lower priority for some governments.
Reliance on private capital
The IHLEG estimates ~½ the $1.3tn could come from
private sources. Private capital is looking for regulation,
insurance and more guarantees to reduce risk.

### Page 8

TO P I C  3 | AD AP TATI O N  &  R ES I L I EN C E (1  o f 2 )
Adaptation will be one of the most important, and more divisive, negotiating topics
Adaptation is also set to be on the formal negotiating agenda at COP31. UN warnings that an overshoot of the Paris Agreement’s 1.5°C goal is now unavoidable (at least temporarily), have
dampened morale for climate activists. As it relates to the COP agenda on adaptation, parties appear deadlocked on whether the COP30 goal to triple adaptation finance by 2035 should be

included in the Global Goal on Adaptation (GGA) text. This is important, as anchoring it to the GGA could signal to investors the scale and timing of future public and blended finance flows.
The role of adaptation in the COP process
The Global Goal on Adaptation (GGA) was established under the Paris Agreement to enhance
adaptive capacity, strengthen resilience and reduce vulnerability to climate change. At COP30
last year, countries adopted 59 indicators (still being refined) to measure progress against the

goal. They also agreed a goal to triple adaptation finance by 2035. The pledge was vague: it set
neither a concrete figure nor a baseline year (a potential annual target of $120bn would be
triple the previous goal of c.$40bn by 2025).
Why GGA talks were deadlocked at the June climate meetings
Finance was the main sticking point at the June Bonn meetings. Developing countries wanted
the COP30 goal to triple adaptation finance by 2035 referenced in the GGA text; countries

including Canada, Norway and Japan opposed. Parties also split on whether the indicators task
force should be expert- or party-led, so no GGA text was agreed and the decision passes to
COP31. Moving the Adaptation Fund to serve the Paris Agreement exclusively (funded by a 5%
share of proceeds from Article 6.4 credits) stalled in Bonn over the composition of its board;
talks will resume at COP31.
What is needed at COP31 to unlock adaptation progress

Adaptation is on the provisional COP31 agenda, including the tripling adaptation finance goal
(though this may get blocked). Some parties want it explicitly referenced in the final COP31
GGA decision; this should become clearer when the agenda is adopted as COP31 opens on 9
Nov. At issue: the tripling pledge still lacks a baseline and named contributors. At COP30,
developed nations resisted new finance obligations, while the EU and many others pushed for

a roadmap to transition away from fossil fuels, which a group of mostly oil- and gas-producing
countries blocked. The result was a looser tripling goal and no agreed fossil-fuel roadmap.
Developing countries are unlikely to give ground, as adaptation relies far more on public
funding than mitigation, so a repeat of COP30’s finance vs. mitigation debate is plausible.
Sources: United Nations, UNEP, Carbon Brief, European Parliament (COP30 outcomes briefing, December 2025)

### Page 9

TO P I C  3 | AD AP TATI O N  &  R ES I L I EN C E (2  o f 2 )
The EU’s proposed Climate Resilience Law plans for a 3°C world, while reiterating commitment to net zero 2050
The EU Commission’s recently proposed Climate Resilience Law would set a reference global warming trajectory of 3°C, providing a benchmark for member states’ adaptation planning. The EU
states it remains committed to reaching net zero by 2050, but some at COP will likely view the proposal as accepting overshoot. With the EU also pointing to an estimated need for EUR70bn in

annual adaptation investment, this provides an interesting backdrop as COP31 seeks to resume adaptation negotiations which stalled earlier this year, primarily due to finance.
What the Commission proposed
On 27 October the Commission presented its EU Framework for Climate Resilience, anchored
by a proposed European Climate Resilience Law. With current global policy commitments
pointing to around 2°C of warming by 2050 and 3°C by 2100, the law would set a common

reference trajectory of 1.5°C in 2030, 2°C in 2050 and 3°C in 2100, with this regarded as the
“baseline assumption”. Member states would be required to use it in national climate risk
assessments and adopt a single national resilience plan by end-2030.
Why it matters: the EU is planning for overshoot
The EU highlighted warnings that overshoot of the 1.5°C goal is now “unavoidable” (even if just
temporarily) as necessitating policy action, even as the EU says it is still committed to climate

neutrality by 2050. The Commission links the plan to Europe’s hottest summer on record and
estimates annual economic losses from climate extremes of €50bn over the past four years,
adding that output losses caused by climate change go far beyond this. It says EU adaptation
needs are ~€70bn a year through to 2050, with Europe warming at 2x the global rate.
What it means for COP31
The proposal demonstrates that warnings on the implications of a significant overshoot of the

Paris Agreement global warming limits are moving from scientific theory to real government
policy. While the opening line of the new EU framework emphasized it remains committed to
reaching net zero by 2050, some may view a 3°C planning baseline as accepting overshoot.
The headline €70bn annual EU adaptation finance requirement also provides an interesting
backdrop heading into Global Goal on Adaptation talks given they are stalled largely due to

disagreement over explicit links to finance in the GGA text. For context, the UNEP estimates
annual adaptation needs for developing countries of $310–365bn by 2035. Ultimately, we
expect adaptation focus to continue growing as warming impacts are increasingly felt and
societal calls for relief grow louder. Corporates will need to consider potential strategic
impacts, while some investors have already begun raising their baseline warming forecasts.
